General Safety Guidelines                                          Power Supply
                                                                   •   Only use the power cable that came with Iridium.
s Please read the following safety tips carefully! They            •   Unplug the device when you are not using it for longer
     include several precautions you should always ob-
                                                                       periods.
     serve when dealing with electronic equipment. Read
     all of the instructions before operating your device.         •   Never touch the plug with wet hands.

Suitable Operating Conditions                                      •   Always pull the plug when unplugging the device -
                                                                       never the cable.
•   Use the device in enclosed rooms only.
                                                                   Operation
•   Never use the device in damp conditions such as
    bathrooms, washrooms, or around indoor swimming                •   Never place objects containing liquids on or near the
    pools.                                                             device.
•   Do not use the device in extremely dusty or dirty en-          •   Place the device on a stable base only. Use a suitable
    vironments.                                                        platform.
•   Make sure that adequate ventilation is available on all        •   Make sure no foreign objects find their way into the
    sides of the device.                                               chassis. If for some reason this occurs, switch the
                                                                       power off, unplug the device, and consult a qualified
•   Do not place the device near heat sources such as radi-
                                                                       repair center.
    ators.
                                                                   •   This device can generate volume levels that may do
•   Do not expose the device to direct sunlight.
                                                                       irreparable damage to your hearing when used on its
•   Do not expose the device to extreme vibrations.                    own or with amplifiers, speakers, or headphones. For
                                                                       this reason you should keep the volume at tolerable le-
                                                                       vels.



                                                              11                                              Iridium Manual
General Safety Guidelines


Maintenance
•   Do not open the device or remove the cover. Refer all
    service and repair tasks to qualified personnel. The in-
    terior of the chassis contains no components that re-
    quire user maintenance.
•   Use only a dry, soft cloth or brush to clean the device.
    Never use alcohol, cleaning solutions or similar chemi-
    cals. They will damage the surface of the chassis.

Proper Use
This device is designed exclusively to produce low-
frequency audio signals for the purpose of generating
sound. Any other use is prohibited and voids the warranty
extended by Waldorf Music. Waldorf Music is not liable for
damages due to incorrect use.

s Please do not press and hold the Global, Master and
     Load buttons during start up procedure. This will
     activate the Skynet artificial intelligence. Waldorf is
     not responsible for further courses of the timeline.

Physical Security (Iridium Desktop only)
Users operating Iridium in areas with public or
shared access such as live gigs, public studios or
educational establishments can attach a securi-
ty lock to the slot on the rear panel of Iridium.

What is polyphonic aftertouch?                                      The Play Button Section for Iridium Desktop
In the case of electronic keyboards such as synthesizers,
aftertouch (or also known as key pressure) is the term
used to describe the control by pressure exerted on the
keyboard. Pressing a key more or less after the actual key
                                                                    This section offers four buttons regarding playback and
press continuously generates an additional modulation
                                                                    apreggiator/sequencer functionality.
signal that is used to modulate tone or timbre - such as the
pitch, the intensity of a vibrato, the volume, but also the
                                                                    Mono Button
waveform or filter used.
                                                                    Switches polyphonic to monophonic mode and vice versa.
Especially when using MIDI devices, it is important to
                                                                    If the Mono button lights up (or blinks depending on the
differentiate between two types of aftertouch:
                                                                    Button Backlight setting, see Global -> Colors page for
•   Monophonic aftertouch ("Channel Aftertouch"), where             more information), the monophonic mode is active. Mono
    the pressure value has the same effect on all notes             means that only the last played note sounds. All other
    played anf transmitted in the respective MIDI channel.          notes are stored in an internal list but aren’t played. As
•   Polyphonic aftertouch, that is transmitted individually         soon as you release the note that is currently played, the
    for each note. For example, you can play and hold 10            second last note is played and so on. When you play legato,
    keys at a time and move your fingers to create poly-            only the first note that was played triggers those envelopes
    phonic aftertouch data. Each finger creates its own da-         which have the SingleTrig parameter activated. Those
    ta stream for modulation purposes.                              envelopes with SingleTrig deactivated will re-trigger at
                                                                    each note even when played legato. You can freely mix and
As you can probably imagine, the Iridium Keyboard offers

Latch Button                                                         The 16 Pads and Mode Buttons of Iridium
If active, all played notes are held. This also affects the          Desktop
arpeggiator. The Latch button acts similar to a pressed
                                                                     Here, you find four buttons to determine the behaviour of
sustain pedal, but in addition you could release individual
                                                                     the 16 pads. Based on the selected mode (Notes, Scale,
latched notes by pressing them again. Press Latch again to
                                                                     Chords, Trigger), the pads offer a different functionality
disable it.
                                                                     when hit.
Arpeggiator (Arp) Button
Push this button to open the Arpeggiator display page
within the Autoplay tab. To start/stop the arpeggiator,
press the Play button below the Tempo dial. When you hit
a note or a chord on the keyboard or via MIDI, it is split up
and repeated rhythmically.

r    In Latch mode, all played notes are held until their
     key is pushed again.

Sequencer (Seq) Button
Push this button open the Step Sequencer display page
within the Autoplay tab. When you hit a note or a chord on
the keyboard or via MIDI, it is repeated rhythmically based
on the entered steps.
                                                                     r    Keep in mind that each layer has its own pad confi-
r    Read more about the Arpeggiator and the Sequencer                    guration. The pads will have the pad configuration of
     in ‘The Perform Mode’ chapter.                                       the active layer. Everything will be saved with a
                                                                          patch.

Setup and Connections


Tempo and Play Button of Iridium Desktop
With the Tempo dial, you can determine the
tempo for a selected sound programm. It
affects the arpeggiator and sequencer, beat-
synced LFOs and other tempo-based effects
Press the Play button to start or stop the
arpeggiator or sequencer for the selected
sound program.

Sound Parameters



Sound Parameters
Overview of Functions
The Iridium consists of numerous sound-shaping compo-
nents. The following manual pages describe all parameters
in detail.
Note that Iridium consists of two different types of com-
ponents for sound generation and sound shaping:
•   Sound synthesis: Oscillator models, Filters, Digital
    Former, Amplifier, Effects. These modules represent
    the audio signal flow. Sound generation actually occurs
    within the oscillators. They can produce different sha-                   Potential signal routing for an audio signal
    pes based on the selected model – e.g. classic wave-
                                                                      •   Modulators: LFOs, Envelopes, Komplex Modulator,
    forms such as square, sawtooth, triangle, and sine, or
                                                                          Modulation Matrix. These modules are called Modula-
    wavetables and samples. The dual filters shape the
                                                                          tors. The Modulators are designed to manipulate or
    sound by amplifying (boosting) or attenuating (dam-
                                                                          modulate the sound generating components to add dy-
    pening) certain frequencies. Digital Former delivers
                                                                          namics to sounds. The Low-frequency Oscillators
    additional filter models and effects for sound proces-
                                                                          (LFOs) are designed for periodic or recurring modula-
    sing. The Amplifier and the Effects are located at the
                                                                          tions while Envelopes are normally used for modulati-
    end of the signal chain. They determine the overall vo-
                                                                          ons that only occur once on each note. These genera-
    lume of the signal and add up to five effects like chorus,
                                                                          tors are assigned to parameters through the Modulati-
    flanger, delay, and reverb, etc.
                                                                          on Matrix and influence these parameters to alter a
                                                                          sound.




                                                                 33                                               Iridium Manual
Sound Parameters


Oscillator Section                                           Iridium offers three oscillators with different capacities:
                                                             Wavetable Oscillator, Waveform Oscillator, Particle
                                                             Generator, Resonator, and Kernel synthesis.
                                                             The most important oscillator controls can be found in the
                                                             Oscillator section on the Iridium’s user panel.
                                                             Choose the desired oscillator model by pressing the cor-
                                                             responding button. To enter the Kernel mode, press Wave-
                                                             table and Waveform buttons simultaneously. The button
                                                             of the chosen model lights up. Every model uses its own
                                                             color – as is the case for the oscillator parameter knobs.
                                                             For example, if the Particle Generator for Oscillator 1 is
                                                             selected, the whole section lights up in blue.

                                                             r    If you press an active oscillator model button, the
                                                                  current oscillator is switched off completely. This gi-
                                                                  ves you a better overview when editing sounds be-
                                                                  cause you can use it as a mute function.

                                                             r    For each synthesis type, we have included Presets
                                                                  with which you can quickly create your own sounds
                                                                  and explore the possibilities of synthesis.
                                                             Based on the selected model the oscillator parameter
                                                             knobs have different functions. This will be explained in
      The Iridium Oscillator Section for Oscillator 1        detail in the next chapters.

    responding oscillator 1, 2, or 3. The

A wavetable is a table consisting of single cycle waveforms.
Each waveform is classified by its own special sound cha-           r    Further parameters can be found on the correspon-
racter. The main difference of wavetable synthesis in com-               ding Oscillator display page. Read more on the over-
parison with other sound-generation principles is the                    next page.
ability to not only to play one waveform per oscillator but
also to step through the wavetable via different modulati-          Wavetable Oscillator Panel Parameters
ons, thereby creating wavetable sweeps. The results can be
dramatic – much more so than anything any sample play-
back-based system could ever produce.                               Semitone / Octave

    changes.                                                        chorus-like effect. Use a positive setting for one oscillator

    connected MicroSD card/USB storage device. This
    audio file will be automatically synthesized as a new

•   Load Single Cycle: Load a single-cycle waveform from
    the internal Flash memory (Internal) or a connected
    MicroSD Card/USB storage device into the Iridium’s
    Flash memory. A single-cycle waveform is in fact a very
    short wav/aif file which samples are interpreted as a
    single cycle of a wave. Good sizes are from 200 to 1024
    samples. The size can be odd. You could use longer files
    but waveform will be averaged to a maximum of 1024
    samples in case. An 8 position wavetable will be crea-
    ted morphing the single-cycle wave into a sine.

Waveform Oscillator Display Page
To access the Waveform Oscillator display mode, press the
corresponding OSC 1, 2 or 3 button above the touchscreen
display.
Here you can switch between three modes: Pitch & Tools,
Mix, and Timbre. Tap on the corresponding tab to select it.
The display switches to this mode. A fourth tab displays
the current oscillator model. Tap on it to select another
model or to switch it off.
Pitch & Tools Mode Tab
Here you find parameters relating to tuning and some
basic settings. The functions and options are the same as            Count
for the Wavetable oscillator. Please refer to the correspon-
ding chapter.                                                        Same functionality as for the corresponding panel parame-
                                                                     ter.

Phase
Determines the start phase of the selected oscillator shape.
•   Running results in free-running oscillators as in ana-
    log synthesizers. A played note „jumps in“ at the cur-
    rent phase cycle.
•   Reset forces the oscillator waveform to start with
    phase 0 for every new note played. Use this setting if
    you want to create drum-like sounds where every note
    sounds similar during its attack phase.
Tap on Phase to open a pop-up menu for further settings:

Stereo Width
The grain streams are panned to left/right based on the
setting of this parameter. It determines the position of the
granular streams in the stereo field. The lower the value,
the less the stereo width (0% is mono).
Tap on Stereo Width to open a pop-up menu for further
settings:
•   Normal: Stereo Width change is about 1.0 %.
•   Fine: Stereo Width change is about 0.1%.

There are two levels of working with the Kernel mode: The            groups of people: Most just browse fixed presets, but a few
Template mode as described below and the real deep                   will do deep sound programming work. We wanted to
editing mode.                                                        create a creative middle ground, to provide an inspiring
                                                                     sound design without becoming an FM wizzard.
The idea of the template mode is to provide a mezzanine
level in which you can focus on your sound design and                But some fearless might want to press “Edit”:
music by just using a few macro parameters. In fact these
are the 5 main pots each oscillator has on the panel. Since          Edit
each template uses them differently, their names and set-            Tap on the Edit button to open the deeper editing level of
tings are presented on the main screen.                              the Kernel mode.
You can use the pre-installed templates, but you can also
build your own. Technically, templates are nothing more
than read-only presets. So, you could create a new templa-
te by doing some deep edits on the Kernel level and then
going back to the template screen and saving it as an Oscil-
lator preset. Then you can use this preset in another oscil-
lator or in another sound design session as your own start-
ing point. You can also save a preset without deep editing,
just to save the 5 pot settings for a particular template for
later re-use.
This template system is the reason why we call the kernel
mode an “Oscillator Construction Kit.” Some might use the
template level only and will never feel the need to go dee-
per, and that’s perfectly fine. We believe that traditional

The Mod Page                                                          r    Please keep in mind that the amount of modulation
Tap on one the Mod button to switch to the corresponding                   is determined by the level of the modulating kernels.
Kernel display page. This page is identical for all of the six             This is a central and important concept in this type
kernels.                                                                   of synthesis.

                                                                      K1...6 ModType: There are 5 modulation types available:
                                                                      •   Phase FM known historically as simply FM (Frequency
                                                                          Modulation). The modulating kernel modulates the
                                                                          phase of the target (or carrier) kernel. This can have
                                                                          huge sonic effects which are mainly determined by the
                                                                          level of the modulation and their pitch ratio. That me-
                                                                          ans that once you have selected the modulating kernel
                                                                          here, you should change to the modulating kernel and
                                                                          edit its level and pitch ratio to control the sound.
                                                                      •   True FM modulates the actual frequency (and not the
                                                                          phase like in traditional phase-based FM). Compared to
                                                                          Phase FM the sonic effect is even more drastic and can
             The Mod display page for Kernel 1                            quickly get very nasty. If that is what you want, this is
                                                                          your mode.
The following parameters are available:
                                                                      •   Ring does classic ring modulation between modulating
K1...6 ModSrc 1/2/3: Each kernel can have up to 3 other                   kernel and target kernel. The sonic effect is mostly de-
kernels as direct kernel modulation sources (not to be                    termined by the pitch difference of both kernels. Turn
confused with conventional modulation from the modula-                    up both levels to get a strong sound. Ring modulation is
tion matrix and such). In the case of multiple modulation                 also a typical scenario where you could set the modula-
sources their signals will be simply summed.                              ting kernel to a fixed frequency.


                                                                 84                                                Iridium Manual
Sound Parameters


•   AM is similar to Ring with the main sonic difference              Since the level especially of the modulating kernels is of
    that lowering the level of the modulating kernel keeps            extreme sonic importance, each kernel has its own dual-
    more and more of the sound of the target kernel. So, in           decay-stage envelope. The attack is curved while the other
    contrast to Ring, AM blends more in.                              stages are linear.
•   WT Pos: Here, the modulating kernel modulates the
    wavetable position of the target kernel (which obvi-
    ously should have a wavetable set on the Wave page).
    Unless the modulating kernel is in LFO mode, this WT
    Pos modulation is at audio rate and opens a whole ran-
    ge of new, strange and unknown sonic territory. This is
    also a good place to use the Sub-Ratio mode for the
    modulating kernel.
K1...6 Feedback: When in Phase FM or AM mode, a kernel
can modulate itself. In FM this is a classic way to quickly
add partials to the sound in mostly a sawtooth-like
direction, but depending on the amount and level it can
quickly go into harsh noise up to white-noise like sonic
territory. Use it with care, but it is mega-fun since potenti-                         Envelope page for Kernel 1

                    Controls display page
Each knob macro can affect up to 6 kernel parameters
(“Slots”) whether they are used in one or multiple kernels
simultaneously. You can set the target parameter and
amount for each slot. The sonic results can be hugely sa-
tisfying when a single knob movement controls a whole
range of kernel parameters. Please check out the various
templates for ideas ofwhat can be achieved here and then
create your own ones.                                                      Example: Macro assignment for Simple FM template

About Kernel Modulations                                            •   Tap on Patch and then on Algo if not already on the
                                                                        Algorithm page.
In kernel synthesis, there are 3 levels of modulation: First
of course, is the modulation of one kernel to another. This         •   There is already a 2-Op FM algo created by INIT. So,
is what defines kernel synthesis. Second, are the knobs or              either done here or, for the sake of the learning experi-
template macro parameters which modulate up to 6 indi-                  ence, tap on Init to Single to start from scratch.
vidual kernel parameters simultaneously.
                                                                    •   Select K1 and then Mod to set the modulation source.
So, these irst two modulation levels are all happening
inside the kernel oscillator. The third level is simply the         •   Select K2 in K1 ModSrc 1 (top left encoder).
fact that the 5 knob macros are available in the Iridium            •   Select K2 from above to edit kernel 2.
modulation matrix as modulation targets. With these, you

Oscillator Mixer (OSC MIX) Section
In the Oscillator Mixer section you control
the volumes of the three oscillators. If an
oscillator volume dial is turned fully counter-
clockwise, no signal is passed.

Level Osc 1 / Osc 2 / Osc 3
Determines the volume of the corresponding
oscillator.

Routing Button
Press the Routing button to enter the Rou-
ting display page. Read more about signal
routing in the corresponding section of the
Filter chapter.

                                                                     Filter Panel Parameters

                                                                     Cutoff 1 & 2
                                                                     Controls the cutoff frequency for the selected filter types.
                                                                     You can bring more movement into the sound by modula-
                                                                     ting the cutoff frequency via LFOs, filter envelope or the
                                                                     Keytrack parameter of the filter. The functionality of these
                                                                     knobs depends on the Type setting.

                                                                     Resonance 1 & 2
                                                                     Controls the emphasis of the frequencies around the cutoff
                                                                     point. Use lower values to give more brilliance to the
                                                                     sound. At higher values the sound exhibits typical filter
                                                                     characteristics with a strong boost around the cutoff fre-
                                                                     quency. When the setting is raised to maximum, the filter
                                                                     starts to self-oscillate, generating a pure sine wave. This
                                                                     feature can be used to create analog-style effects and per-
                   The Dual Filter Section

nality of these knobs depends on the Type setting.                      This leads to a harsh driven audio signal.

    Cutoff 1. This leads to a boosted signal. Resonance 2

•   Twin Peaks: both filter cutoff values are set to a               part of the display you can call up the Dual Filter 1 and 2

    than Cutoff 2. Cutoff 2 and Resonance 2 have no in-              the desired tab to select the corresponding page.

•   Escaping: both filter cutoff values are set to a constant
    frequency distance where Cutoff 2 is higher than Cutoff
    1. Cutoff 2 and Resonance 2 have no influence here.
•   Opposition: Cutoff 1 controls the frequency of Filter 1
    and 2. Higher values increase Cutoff 1 and decrease
    Cutoff 2. Lower values increase Cutoff 2 and decrease
    Cutoff 1. Cutoff 2 and Resonance 2 have no influence
    here.
•   Endless: similar to Opposition, but cutoff frequency
    from Filter 2 has a different behaviour relationship.
    Cutoff 2 and Resonance 2 have no influence here.
•   Independent: both filters allow independent settings
    for Cutoff 1 and 2 and Resonance 1 and 2.                        r    Digital Former and Routing will be explained in
                                                                          the corresponding chapters.
•   Linked: both filters allow independent settings for
    Cutoff 1 and 2 and Resonance 1 and 2, but Cutoff 1
    controls the overall frequency cutoff.

Env Button
Tap on this botton to switch the Envelope mode on or off.
If inactive (button is greyed out), Filter Level and Filter
Pan can be controlled by the right display parameter dials.
If active (button lits and a small filter envelope graphic is
shown), Filter Envelope Amount and Filter Velocity
Amount can be controlled by the right display parameter
dials.

                                                                  Type
                                                                  The following Digital Former types are available. Depen-
                                                                  ding on the selected type, Amount and Color control diffe-
                                                                  rent settings. This will be explained in the following list:
                                                                  •   Bypass: no effect is used. Amount and Color have no
                                                                      effect on the signal.
                                                                  •   Drive: adds a drive effect. Amount determines the
                                                                      amount of saturation that is added to the signal. If set
                                                                      to 0.00, no saturation will be added – in other words,
                                                                      the signal will remain clean. Lower values will add so-
                                                                      me harmonics to the signal, resulting in a warm charac-
                                                                      ter. Increasing the value will bring in more and more
                                                                      distortion, suitable for harder lead sounds and effects.

Envelopes Section                                                    •    Three additional Free Envelopes. These envelopes
                                                                          can be used freely to perform additional modulations
                                                                          on any module.
                                                                     Most traditional synthesizers feature ADSR envelopes.
                                                                     These envelopes are made up of four parameters that
                                                                     determine their response: Attack, Decay, Sustain, and
                                                                     Release.
                                                                     An envelope is started by pressing a key. It ascends to its
                                                                     maximum value at the rate determined by the Attack
                                                                     parameter. It then descends at the rate determined by the
                                                                     Decay value until it reaches the predetermined Sustain
         The Iridium Keyboard Envelopes Section                      value. It remains at this value until the key is released. The
Iridium’s envelopes allow you to manipulate sound para-              envelope then descends to zero at the rate determined by
meters via rate or timed modulations. Iridium offers six             the Release parameter
independent programmable envelopes for every sound
program:                                                             Envelope Panel Parameters for Iridium Keyboard
•    Two Filter Envelopes. These envelopes are designed
     to control the Dual Filter 1 and 2 but can also be used
                                                                     r    The Panel parameter knobs control the Filter 1 and
                                                                          the Amplifier envelopes. All other envelopes can be
     for other modulations.
                                                                          edited via the touchscreen display.
•    An Amplifier Envelope (Amp). This envelope is de-
     signed to control the sound volume, but can also be             Attack
     used for other modulations.
                                                                     Determines the attack rate or amount of time it takes for a
                                                                     signal to go from zero to maximum level.

                                                                      Mod Targets Button
Single Trigger                                                        Read more in the ‘Modulation’ chapter.
All envelopes offer a single trigger function. It works in
Mono mode only, which can be activated with the Mono
button:
•   When set to Off, every note starts the envelope of its
    own voice; also works when playing legato.
•   When set to On, the envelopes of all voices of a selected
    patch behave like a single envelope. This is the perfect
    setting for playing legato. The common envelope starts
    as soon as the first note is played. The sustain level
    remains until the last note is released. Afterwards the
    release phase is active.
Tap on Single Trig to open a pop-up menu for further
settings:

Speed                                                                Phaser
If Sync is deactivated, the modulation speed can be set
freely in Hertz. If Sync is activated, the parameter name is
called Sync Ratio and allows you to specify the base note
value for tempo-syncing the effect (from 1024 beats to
1/32, straight, triplet, or dotted).
LR Phase Diff
Sets the phase offset of the modulation LFO between the
left and right audio channels. Higher values lead to a wider
stereo range.
Shape
Determines the shape of the LFO waveform, which is used
for the tremolo modulation. You can select between a sine,
a triangle and a square waveform.                                    Phase shifting is a sweeping effect that was first popula-
                                                                     rized in the 1960s. It adds motion and a swirling character
Smooth                                                               to the sound. It works by splitting the incoming signal,
Determines the slew rate of the selected LFO waveform.               changing the phase of one side, and recombining it with
The higher the setting, the smoother the slew rate.                  the unaffected signal. This creates a notch-comb filter that
                                                                     can be swept through the frequency spectrum, causing the
Sync                                                                 signature ‘whooshing’ sound of the phase shifter. The
Synchronizes the delay to the internal tempo of the Iridi-           sweep is caused when the phase of the affected half is
um. If activated, you can set up Sync Ration in musical              modulated by an LFO, with the frequency determined by
values.                                                              Speed. Depth sets the amplitude for the action of the fil-
                                                                     tering, while Feedback amplifies certain harmonics.

Reverb                                                               Time

The Reverb effect is probably the most widely used effect            Determines the reverb time. Lower settings simulate a
in music production. Iridium’s reverb effect is an addition          normal room while higher settings simulate a big hall or
to the sound to make it more expressive with a three-                church.
dimensional feel.                                                    Color
                                                                     Determines the spectral colorization of the reverb sound.
                                                                     Negative values dampen the higher frequencies while
                                                                     positive settings dampen the lower frequencies.
                                                                     Predelay
                                                                     Determines the delay between the direct sound and the
                                                                     reverb effect output. Lower settings connect the reverb
                                                                     more to the original signal while higher settings separate
                                                                     the effect signal to produce a more spacious sound.
                                                                     LR Xfade
                                                                     Crossfades the left and right channel of the reverb effect. It
                                                                     is possible to crossfade from left to right and vice versa.

This parameter controls the volume of the reverb effect. If          Modulation allows you to enrich the reverb flag over sub-

no effect can be heard. Higher values will increase the              quency of this pitch modulation.
reverb level. At maximum setting of 100% the pure reverb
effect signal will be heard.

Compressor                                                            Threshold

An Compressor reduces signals which exceed a certain                  Determines to which signal level the compressor will be
Threshold level in volume by an adjustable Ratio. The                 working. Only signal levels above threshold are processed.
speed of this level reduction is controlled by Attack, when           Input Gain
the signal starts to exceed the threshold, and Release,
when it drops below the threshold again.                              Determines the input gain of the incoming audio signal.
                                                                      Attack
                                                                      Determines how fast the compressor responds to audio
                                                                      signals above the set Threshold. If the attack time is high,
                                                                      more of the transient parts of the signal passes through
                                                                      unprocessed.
                                                                      Release
                                                                      Sets the time after which the gain returns to its original
                                                                      level when the signal drops below the threshold.
                                                                      Output Gain
                                                                      Compensates for output gain loss caused by compression.
                                                                      Auto Gain
Ratio
                                                                      Switch on the Auto Gain function for an automatic control
Sets the amount of gain reduction (compression) applied               of Output Gain when adjusting the Threshold.
to signals above the set threshold. A ratio of 4:1 means that
for every 4 dB the input level increases, the output level
increases by 1 dB.

Look Ahead
Higher Look Ahead values produce more accurate proces-
sing, but adds a certain amount of latency.

r    Keep in mind, that the Compressor effect settings
     are stored with the current sound program. This is
     not valid for the Compressor knob in the Output
     section.
Tap on the corresponding effect parameter to open a pop-
up menu for further settings (not all settings are available
for all effect parameters):
•   Normal: parameter change is about 1%.
•   Fine: parameter change is about 0.1%.
•   Super Fine: parameter change is about 0.01%.
•   Set Default: sets the parameter to its default value.
•   Set Last Loaded: resets the parameter to the last
    loaded value from the current sound patch.
•   Modulations: opens the Mod menu to sets up a modu-
    lation for this parameter. More information can be
    found in ‘The Modulation Mode’ chapter.
•   MIDI Learn CC: activates MIDI Learn, which maps an
    incoming MIDI control change to this parameter.

The Modulations of Iridium                                             The display shows the Modulation Assignment window.
                                                                       The basic concept is to select a modulation destination
A modulation can be described as a signal-generating                   first (a parameter or function to be modulated), then select
unit’s influence upon a sound parameter. The terms used                a modulation source (a function, such as an LFO or a
in this context are ‘Source’ and ‘Destination’. Iridium offers         wheel, that will modulate the destination) and finally de-
40 independent modulation assignments (slots) each with                termine the amount of modulation. This is also called mo-
individual settings of source, destination, and amount. The            dulation depth or intensity.
Modulation Matrix (Mod Matrix) is key to the power of
every Waldorf synthesizer, so start experimenting with it              r    If you have opened the Modulation Assignment
right now.                                                                  window by clicking on a display parameter, the mo-

     tions can be found in the Appendix.

nes, and blurring sounds.                                            Komplex Modulator signal.

                                                                     Blend
                                                                     Mixes the result of Curve A and Curve B. The leftmost
                                                                     setting (A 100%) only uses the signal from Curve A as
                                                                     modulation source while the rightmost setting (B 100%)
                                                                     only uses Curve B. Other settings morph Curve A into
                                                                     Curve B, and vice versa.

Bass Boost
Activates/deactivates the typical Waldorf bass emphasis
for the overall audio signal.

Timbre Mode
Iridium offers three different modes for the Sound layers:
•   Single: both Sound layers have individual settings and
    cannot be played together. You can switch between
    two layers by tapping on the Layer 1 or Layer 2 but-
    ton in the touchscreen display.

Controller (only for Split and Layered Mode)
Determines which Layer will be affected by incoming Mod
wheel, Pitch bend, Aftertouch, Pads and Modulation pad
data.
•   Both: wheels and play controls affect both layers.
•   Layer 1 / Layer 2: wheels and play controls affect
    Layer 1 or Layer 2.
•   Selected: wheels and play controls affect the current
    selected layer.

The Arpeggiator
An Arpeggiator is a device that splits an incoming chord
into its individual notes and repeats them rhythmically.
Different sequence modes can be defined for the Arpeggia-
tor to cover a wide range of applications. In addition to the
synthesis features Iridium offers a programmable arpeg-
giator. It can play a wide range of different rhythm pat-
terns.
To enter the Arpeggiator, tap on the Mode button and                            The Autoplay tab for Iridium Keyboard
select Arp. You can alternatively press the Arp | Seq button          Bpm
Iridium Keyboard) or the Arp button (Iridium Desktop).
                                                                      Sets the overall tempo for the Arpeggiator and the Se-
r    In Latch mode, all played notes are held until their             quencer in Bpm (beats per minute). This tempo also affects
     key is pushed again.                                             the functions that may depend on tempo settings (e.g.
                                                                      LFOs, Komplex Modulator, Delay effect).
r    The arpeggiator outputs MIDI notes in case the MIDI
     Out function on the Global menu page is enabled.                 r     Iridium Desktop: This parameter can also be con-
                                                                            trolled with the Tempo dial.

                                                                  180                                                 Iridium Manual
Additional Modes


tions of beats. For example, if Step Length is set to 1/8, the
sequence plays eight notes per measure.

Swing
Determines how much the timing affects a sequence step.
If Swing is set to 0.50, the sequence is played back without
any shuffled timing. Settings from 0.51 to 0.95 increase the
shuffling of the notes and create a typical swing. You can
also use settings below 0.50 to influence the timing.

In this mode the 4x4 pads in the display will be sequential-           XY Tab
ly run through based on the direction given by the Algo-
rithm parameter. The pitches of each pad and therefore of              This page provides an X-Y pad, a two-dimensional control-
the sequenced steps will be taken from either the Notes,               ler based on two selected modulation parameters.
Scale or Chords pitch configurations of the pads. Press the            First you need to define the X and Y controllers within the
respective button (Iridium Desktop) or tab (Iridium Key-               Modulation Matrix. You can, for example, easily set up a
board) for setting up these pitches.                                   modulation for Cutoff (Pad X) and Resonance (Pad Y).
If no pad is latched all display pads will be used to se-
quence the notes. For rhythmic variation you can use the
parameters Rhythm Pattern, Swing and Rel Gate.
You could also press the Latch button and then select a set
of steps by tapping on a corresponding pad. This is high-
lighted in white and only this will be used for playback, i.e.
when the algorithm is wandering through the pads only
the latched ones will play. For the other ones a rest will be
inserted. If you like to get back using all pads in playback,
then simply press Latch to un-latch everything.

upper display area. On the next pages, we describe every                the Input signal of the external audio input as well as

                                                                    •   Type: you can choose three types of graphic represen-
Scope Tab                                                               tation: Scope shows a realtime stereo wavemeter (see
Here you can select a graphic representation of the current             also Mode), Analyzer (lin), and Analyzer (log) shows a
sound that is played. Tap on Source, Type, or Mode to                   graphic analyzer of the frequency spectrum of the sig-
open a pop-up menu with further settings.                               nal, either with a linear (lin) or a logarithmic (log) re-
                                                                        presentation. Spectrogram shows the spectrum of fre-
                                                                        quencies of the audio signal as it varies with time.
                                                                    •   Mode/FFT Size: depends on the selected Type. If
                                                                        Scope is selected, you can choose between One Shot (a
                                                                        momentary shot of the corresponding sound), Pitch (a
                                                                        continuous representation) and Continuous (a realtime
                                                                        wavemeter). If any other Type is chosen, it determines
                                                                        the block size of the FFT (Fast Fourier Transformation)
                                                                        spectrum analyzer. Higher block sizes provide more
                                                                        resolution in the lower frequency range, but decrease
                                                                        time coherence (time precision) in the higher fre-
                                                                        quency range – the higher frequency information
                                                                        becomes over-averaged.

    for MIDI note A3. The default setting is 440 Hz, which
    is commonly used by most instruments.

                                                                   About MPE
                   The MIDI Inputs page                            MIDI Polyphonic Expression (or MPE) is a method of using
Inputs                                                             MIDI to enable expressive electronic musical instruments
                                                                   to control multiple dimensions of sound polyphonically. In
Here you can change settings regarding the MIDI inputs             MIDI, channel-wide messages (such as pitch bend, CCs, and
(DIN In, USB Controller In, USB Computer In):                      channel aftertouch) are applied to all notes being played
                                                                   on a single channel; therefore, in MPE each note is assig-


                                                             200                                              Iridium Manual
Additional Modes


ned its own channel so that those messages can be applied             Iridium supports an optional second MPE zone; channel
to each note individually.                                            ranges can be configured with the Channel button (when
                                                                      MPE is set to active) or set automatically from MCM mes-
An MPE instrument typically has three dimensions of
                                                                      sages. To use dual MPE zones it is recommended to choose
expression/control: left-right (X-axis); front-back (Y-axis);
                                                                      the “Split” layer mode.
and pressure (Z-axis) – each axis can be mapped to various
parameters of sound and be applied on a per-note basis.
                                                                      Outputs
Each MPE dimension is mapped to the Iridium sound engi-
ne as follows:                                                        Here you can make settings relating to the MIDI outputs
                                                                      (DIN Out, USB Controller Out, USB Computer Out).
•   Left-right / X-axis (sent as MIDI Pitch Bend) – This
    controls the pitch of the note, with the range set using
    the PB Range for the corresponding oscillator.
•   Front-back / Y-axis (sent as MIDI CC 74) – This con-
    trols the destination parameter set for the modulation
    source MPE Y Axis.
•   Pressure / Z-axis (sent as MIDI Channel Aftertouch) –
    this controls the Aftertouch modulation assignments.
From now on, MPE support is available for MIDI DIN, USB
Host (Controller) and USB Device (Computer). You can
activate MPE support individually. Further, if a connected
MPE Controller sends MCM (MPE Configuration Messages),
these will be detected and will automatically put Iridium
into MPE mode.                                                        •   State: Tap on this button to switch the corresponding
                                                                          MIDI Out on or off. Keep in mind that the arpeggiator




                                                                201                                             Iridium Manual
Additional Modes


    and the step Sequencer will send MIDI notes for enab-
    led MIDI outputs.
•   Chan 1 / 2: here you can determine the MIDI channel
    for Layer 1 and 2, on which MIDI data is sent out.
•   Send CC: here you can activate all mapped MIDI Con-
    trol Change (MIDI CC) data being sent out for the panel
    pots, buttons, and encoder.
•   Send NRPN: if set to On, the physical panel elements
    send out MIDI NRPN data. For parameter automation
    please use MIDI CC as before. Keep in mind, that the
    Iridium doesn´t react on incoming NRPN MIDI data.

                                                                  Opens a page containing all mapped MIDI Control Change
Determines the overall tempo for the Iridium in Bpm               data. This page gives you an overview of all used MIDI
(beats per minute). This tempo is valid for all functions         mappings. You can also change or set up settings.

Tap on Bpm a pop-up menu for further settings:
•   Normal: Bpm changes with normal values.
•   Fine: Bpm changes with fine values.
•   Super Fine: Bpm changes with super fine values.
•   Set Default: sets Bpm to its default value of 120.0
•   Set Last Loaded: resets the parameter to the last
    loaded value from the current sound patch.
•   MIDI Learn CC: activates MIDI Learn, which maps an
    incoming MIDI control change to this parameter.

                                                                   You can also set the CV scale by feeding the appropriate
                                                                   voltage to the CV input and then tap on „Scan 100%“
                                                                   Iridium is always capable to receive CV from -12V to +12V
                                                                   even when the CV scale is much less, voltages above the CV
                                                                   scale value will no be cut off.
                                                                   For 1V/Oct pitch control (which is a standard for all Eu-
                                                                   rorack modules/systems) set CV Scale around 2V and the
                                                                   Mod Amount to 100%. A mod amount of 100% corres-
                                                                   ponds in the mod matrix when modulated to pitch to 24
 CV/Gate and Clock connections on the Iridium rear panel           semitones or 2 octaves. Thus, the recommended value of
                                                                   2V for the CV scale.

CV Start In starts/stops the arpeggiator or sequencer de-          Here you can switch on/off the backlight LEDs for all but-

Gate Input
Analog gate signals can be fed into Gate In. A positive
voltage will gate a new note in Iridium, using a default
pitch of MIDI 60. You can use one of the CV inputs as
described about to control the note's pitch.

g An Example:
     • Route CV1 in the mod matrix to pitch with an
     amount 100%.
     • Set the CV1 scaling to 2V and optionally fine tune
     this value to calibrate a precise octave.
     • Now, 0V at CV1 would correspond to MIDI pitch
     60 aka C3, 1V to C4, 2V to C5 and so forth.
     • You can adjust the base pitch at 0V for each oscil-         •   Button Backlight: here, you can switch on/off the

     on source and an appropriate amount value.                    •   Custom Section Color List: here, you can define a

•   Remove: removes the selected sample from the list.

•   Load Map: loads a Map from the Flash memory.

    Map. Tap on Save Map to perform the store process.
•   Clear Map: deletes all samples and their settings.

The Select Button                                                    Tap on the Close button to return to the Sample page.
Tap on the Select button to open a pop-up menu with
different selection options:
•   All: selects the complete sample.
•   None: deselects any selection of the sample.
•   Start->End: selects the sample area between sample
    start and end point.
•   Loop: selects the sample area between loop start and
    end point.
•   Trim: sets the start and end point around the selection
    which means that the sample start point is set to the
    beginning of the selection and the sample end point to
    the selection end.
•   Set Loop: sets a loop around the selection which me-
    ans that the loop start point is set to the beginning of
    the selection and the loop end point to the selection
    end.

forms over time.                                                      Of course you can combine envelope and keytrack modula-

The decay stage would move through these waves in the                 ons will be added such that the end or the beginning of a
opposite direction prior to holding a certain wave during             wavetable might be exceeded. In such cases, the waves will

Most wavetables are created so that they start with a hol-
low wave at position 0 and go through increasingly brigh-

viour similar to a low pass �ilter so that they can be conve-
ter waves up to maximum position. This results in a beha-

niently controlled by an envelope.
If Attack is 0 and Decay set to a medium value you get a
percussive sound; if you turn up the attack, you get a soft-
sounding start.
You can also use an LFO to modulate the wavetable positi-
on and, depending on the selected LFO Shape, you might
get a wave scan that goes back and forth (triangle), in only
ione direction followed by a hard reset to the origin
(sawtooth) or between only two waves (square).

The Pulse Wave
                                                                                                                             ""'()*+,-
The Pulse wave is the most versatile wave in a classic
synthesizer because its shape and therefore its harmonic
                                                                                                !   "   #   $   % !! !" !#
content can be changed in realtime. This is done by chan-
ging the width of the upper and lower portion of the wave-                                                                   6#'()*+,-
form cycle. These portions are called pulses – hence the
pulse width name. The width of the first pulse is used to
                                                                                                !   "   #   $   % !! !" !#
distinguish between different pulse waves and it is mea-
sured in percent. The following image illustrates several                                                                    7!'()*+,-
pulse waves with different pulse widths:                                                                                     .89:*+,-5

                                                                                                !   "   #   $   % !! !" !#

                                                                       Additive components of Pulse wave with different pulse
                                                                                              widths
                                                                     The first thing you probably observe is that the lower part
                                                                     of the wave has a narrower excursion. This is because the
                                                                     energy of the wider pulse is higher than the one of the
                                                                     narrower pulse. If this were not compensated, the overall
                                                                     signal would have an unwanted DC offset.
                                                                     As you have read in the previous chapter the harmonic
                                                                     content of a 50% pulse wave is a special case. It has a very

special case is a pulse wave with a very narrow pulse                 The Triangle Wave is very similar to the square wave. It is
width – labelled as < 1% in the previous image. An infini-            composed of the same harmonics as the square wave but
tely thin pulse creates a spectrum that has all harmonics             with different magnitude ratios. The magnitude of each
with equal magnitudes. In a digital synthesizer, ‘infinitely’         harmonic is divided by the power of its number. This me-
necessarily means one sample.                                         ans that the third harmonic’s magnitude is a ninth, the fifth
                                                                      harmonic is a twenty-fifth and so on. The following image
The pulse wave is an artificial wave, which means that it             illustrates the harmonic content:
doesn’t occur in nature. It was built into synthesizers be-
cause it could create a lot of different timbres with a mini-
mum of technical effort. However, certain pulse widths
sound very close to the timbres of acoustic (or semi-
acoustic) instruments – e.g. guitar or bass guitar, e-piano,
or even a flute.
The most powerful feature of the pulse wave is the ability
to change its width while sounding. This is called pulse
width modulation. When the pulse width is changed the
waveform starts to sound thicker. This happens because
the effect is very similar to what you hear when you have
two oscillators running with slightly different frequencies.
They interfere and create irregular troughs in the resulting
waveforms.

Granular synthesis is based on a simple idea: instead of

new one starts. To avoid discontinuities in the playback,
                                                                    have significant influence on the Iridium’s sound charac-
and to minimize artefacts, envelopes are applied to the
                                                                    teristics.
grains. Granular synthesis can be used to extract interes-
ting spectra from all kinds of samples creating sound               Now, we’ll explain the basic function of a filter discussing

USB
The Universal Serial Bus (USB) is a serial bus system to
connect a computer with an external device. USB equipped
devices can be plugged together while active. The recogni-
tion is made automatically.

Wave
In this context, a Wave is a digitally-memorized reproduc-
tion of one single wave pass insofar as it is identical to a
sample that is looped after one single wave pass. In con-
trast to the samples in a sampler, all waves in Waldorf
Wavetable synthesizers have the same lengths and are
played back in the same pitch.

Wavetable
One oscillator shape in the Iridium is based on waveform
sets called wavetables. You should think of these as a se-
quence of up to up to 128 single waves. This can be played
back in a static way or played through dynamically, which
results in their typically interesting sound transforma-
tions. If the waves do not differ much, then the wavetable
